 "disposition": "UNDER_INVESTIGATION",
 "spec_asserts": "The channel is append-only and each row carries its own author in surfaced_by; a seat commits its own row. Nothing in the protocol or the gates addressed what a commit of the shared FILE carries beyond the committer's own row.",
 "world_is": "On a shared checkout with roughly six seats appending to one file, committing your own row carries a foreign-row hazard that no seat's own correctness protects it from. It happened three times in four hours to three seats, each of which had a guard that ran and did not cover the case.",
 "notes": "WHY THIS ROW EXISTS AND WHAT IT IS NOT. It adjudicates nothing, asks nothing, and re-grades nothing: no revert, no history rewrite, no re-append, because deleting a peer's filed row to correct an attribution field is the worse trade on an append-only channel and every row's true author was never in doubt. THE PROSE HALF IS REAL AND STAYS A HABIT: two known instances (7abe7552b5 and f5859c4097, both self-disclosed), 8 of 13 narrative-changing commits deliberately unclassified, and this seat's own 11 commits on this file classified as 9 prose-changing with every added line inside the three regions it authored and none elsewhere. WHAT THE ROUND SHOULD KEEP: of the seven messages that carried this class, four were seats correcting their OWN earlier claims after reading someone else's bound, and in three of those the denominator had been printed faithfully while the claim's subject was wider than the thing counted. That pattern is the control that actually worked here."

The correction is offered with its own qualifier, since the live protection value is unmeasured at this seat. TRIAGE, for the issuer: this is not the most urgent thing in the round by data risk, and it IS the cheapest -- either disarm is a single command, and it should ride the respawned lane's FIRST slot exactly as 19820 says, because the window in which the file can be lost is open for as long as the lane is unseated.  "spec_asserts": "TO-OP-S-17R-28 deliverable 1: the new evidence store sends server access logs to opal-data-security-access-logs under its own non-colliding prefix, and S17R-AC3 grades that limb on a DELIVERED OBJECT rather than on the configuration. S17R28-AC3: the apply's changed-resource list contains ONLY the new bucket and its configurations.",
 "world_is": "The log target admits exactly one source bucket by an ArnLike condition written deliberately to exclude every other bucket in the account. Delivery from a second source is refused, and refused delivery is indistinguishable from best-effort latency because both present as an empty prefix. Satisfying the deliverable therefore requires an edit to an existing resource that the acceptance criterion excludes from the apply.",
 "notes": "WHY THIS IS FILED BEFORE THE EDIT RATHER THAN DISCLOSED AFTER IT: the assignee could quietly add the new bucket's ARN to that condition and report a clean delivered object, and nothing in the acceptance would show that an existing resource had been widened -- S17R-AC1 and S17R-AC3 would both read PASS. The never-widen bound and 'no other resource rides' are exactly what would have been consumed silently. TWO SHAPES, both the issuer's call and neither taken here: (a) EXTEND the target's ArnLike to admit the new bucket as a second source -- minimal, stays inside the project's own resources, keeps the same service principal and the same account condition, and preserves the SourceArn control's purpose since it would still name exactly two buckets rather than becoming a wildcard; S17R28-AC3 is then amended to expect the new bucket, its configurations AND that one policy update, enumerated. (b) DROP the logging limb from this face and route it to its own cell, in which case S17R-AC1's fifth read and S17R-AC3 both file UNCOMPUTABLE citing this row and the new bucket ships without access logging. THE ASYMMETRY WORTH NAMING: under (a) the cost is one enumerated extra resource in one apply; under (b) the evidence store runs unlogged until a later cell lands. THE REUSABLE PART: a permission-conditioned delivery path fails as an ABSENCE, so any criterion that grades it on a delivered object must also confirm the deliverer is PERMITTED -- otherwise the UNCOMPUTABLE branch absorbs a permission denial and reports it as latency, and the 24h window expires without anyone learning which it was. That is the same shape as this project's Z-S5, one bucket over. KEEP-WORKING STATE, folded here because the schema has no field for it: YES -- the row is filed and this seat proceeds. The build of the new bucket, its four public-access blocks, SSE-KMS on the project key, versioning and the SecureTransport-only policy are all unaffected by this question and none of them touches an existing resource. The LOGGING limb alone waits on the issuer's shape, and this seat does not pick between (a) and (b) on its own authority."

WHY THIS SEAT DID NOT FIX IT, and the reasoning is the same one that made Z-S49 worth filing: the two available fixes both trade a real property for a cosmetic one. Pinning managed_rule_group_statement version would stop the churn by selecting a specific AWS ruleset VERSION on live edge traffic -- a behavioural change to protection, made to satisfy a plan check. A lifecycle ignore_changes on rule would silence the diff by blinding terraform to the entire rule array, which is the one thing S18-AC2 relies on to prove no existing rule moved. Making the plan clean would in both cases cost more than the dirty plan does. THE OPERATIONAL COST IS BOUNDED AND WORTH STATING PLAINLY: a permanently non-converging root means every future plan on portal-auth carries this 1-change noise, so a REAL future drift on this ACL would have to be spotted inside a diff that is never empty -- that is the actual harm, and it is a detection cost rather than a protection cost. THE REUSABLE PART: an apply returning rc=0 with 'Modifications complete' does NOT establish that terraform and the world agree -- it establishes that the API accepted the call. Only the re-plan tests agreement, which is exactly why S418-AC3 asks for it, and it is why running that check before declaring the leg closed was worth the two extra minutes."

A DEFECT IN WHAT THE MEMBERS SHARE IS INVISIBLE TO A CHECK SCOPED TO THE MEMBERS: nine per-route checks all passed while the property they shared -- an edge-injected key with no edge path for one of them -- went unmeasured, which is CLAUDE.md Rule 22's shared-dependency clause with a live specimen. WHAT I DID NOT MEASURE, so no reader infers it: I did not probe either endpoint (the OP-x-1 list is CLOSED at seven and an eighth probe is not on it), I did not read the access logs, and I therefore CANNOT say whether anything is actually broken -- only that a route is gated on a key that cannot reach it. WHY IT IS FILED AS ARMED ANYWAY: the harm from being wrong is asymmetric and cheap to bound. If a caller exists it has been failing since 16:24:34Z; if none exists the disarm costs one log read. THE PART WORTH KEEPING FOR L9, and it is about the rule rather than the route: a rule stated FORWARD at the moment it is learned leaves every act already taken under the old understanding unexamined, and those are exactly the acts nobody will revisit, because they are CLOSED and their criteria all read PASS. A bar that NAMED its premise -- 'against a pure import', 'against a first create' -- would re-open itself when the shape changed instead of failing closed against work that is correct. That is a cut-time habit, not a gate, and it is the durable output of these three rows."
